Problem Statement &amp; Background</w:r></w:p></w:tc></w:tr></w:tbl><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="100"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">2.1 The Caregiving Gap</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Cognitive decline develops gradually, over months or years. Families miss early warning signs because elderly individuals often live independently or semi-independently, symptoms accumulate slowly, doctor visits are infrequent (typically annual or semi-annual), and existing monitoring solutions are either too invasive (cameras), too unreliable in long-term compliance (wearables that get forgotten or run out of charge), or too infrequent (periodic medical assessments that cannot observe daily life).</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="60"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing Core Innovation â€” The Four-Pillar Attribution Framework</w:r></w:p></w:tc></w:tr></w:tbl><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="100"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.1 Reframing the Problem</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The conventional approach treats multi-person CSI as a signal separation problem: decompose the mixed stream to isolate one person. This is the approach taken by Independent Component Analysis (ICA) and Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) methods, and it is genuinely hard â€” RuView&apos;s own measured result shows cardiac+respiratory channels alone give a similarity gap of only ~0.0005 between two people, i.e. essentially no separation.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="40"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">SilentSense&apos;s reframe: do not separate signals. Instead, build a person-attributed sensing architecture where attribution emerges from converging evidence across four independent channels. No single channel needs to be perfect â€” the system only needs enough channels to agree, often enough, to build a statistically meaningful baseline.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="80"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.2 Pillar 1 â€” Spatial Containment</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Both TX and RX nodes are placed inside the target&apos;s room, on opposite walls. At 2.4GHz, a standard interior wall attenuates signal by approximately 10â€“15 dB. Reflections from people in adjacent rooms therefore arrive at roughly 1/10th to 1/30th the power of in-room motion. The room itself becomes a physical spatial filter â€” the target individual becomes the dominant signal source by physics, not algorithm.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Strength: Highest-confidence pillar; works passively 24/7 with zero compute cost</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Limitation: Does not resolve the case where another person physically enters the target&apos;s room (e.g. a caregiver, a visiting family member)</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="60"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.3 Pillar 2 â€” Temporal Scheduling</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">A probabilistic daily schedule model is built from weeks of occupancy data (using the shared-space occupancy sensor from Layer 1). The model learns, per hour-of-day and per day-of-week, the probability that the target&apos;s room is single-occupant.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Strength: Free â€” requires no additional hardware beyond the occupancy sensor; overnight (11pmâ€“7am) typically yields 8+ hours of near-certain single-occupancy data every night</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Limitation: Breaks down for atypical schedules (irregular caregiver visits, shift-working household members, illness disrupting routine)</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="60"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.4 Pillar 3 â€” Biometric Signature Matching</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">During known single-occupant windows (informed by Pillars 1+2), the system builds an enrolled biometric profile for the target individual: a probability distribution over breathing rate, breathing regularity, and breathing amplitude (reconstructed from CSI). During ambiguous daytime windows, the respiratory-band PSD is analysed for multiple frequency peaks; each peak&apos;s likelihood under the target&apos;s enrolled distribution versus a generic household distribution produces a likelihood ratio.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="40"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:tbl><w:tblPr><w:tblW w:type="dxa" w:w="9360"/><w:tblBorders><w:top people can be near-zero (gap ~0.0005) when rates overlap. Pillar 3 must therefore use the FULL distribution â€” rate, regularity, AND amplitude/depth â€” not a single-value comparison. Elderly breathing differs from younger adults in resting rate (12â€“16 vs 16â€“20 brpm), amplitude (reduced chest wall compliance), and irregularity pattern (micro-arousals). Even with all three dimensions, expect Pillar 3 to be probabilistic enrichment, not deterministic separation.</w:r></w:p></w:tc></w:tr></w:tbl><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Strength: Extends attributable windows beyond Pillars 1+2 alone, particularly for daytime periods when the target is alone in their room but the household is otherwise active</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Limitation: Degrades significantly during motion (breathing signal requires relative stillness); overlapping breathing rates between household members remain a genuine edge case</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="60"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.5 Pillar 4 â€” Behavioural Context Priors</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Uses occupancy-only detection in shared spaces (no identity required there) as a Bayesian prior. If the shared-space sensor shows no activity and the target&apos;s room shows motion, the probability that the motion belongs to the target is very high â€” they are the only person expected to be present when the rest of the household is quiet.</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Strength: Requires no identification infrastructure outside the target&apos;s room â€” identity logic stays concentrated where it matters</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="ListParagraph"/><w:numPr><w:ilvl w:val="0"/><w:numId w:val="2"/></w:numPr><w:spacing w:after="40" w:before="40"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Limitation: A weak prior on its own; most valuable as a multiplier on the other three pillars</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:before="80"/></w:pPr></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:pStyle w:val="Heading2"/><w:spacing w:after="120" w:before="280"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="0F6E56"/><w:sz w:val="26"/><w:szCs w:val="26"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">6.6 Bayesian Fusion Layer</w:r></w:p><w:p><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="100" w:before="60"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Arial" w:cs="Arial" w:eastAsia="Arial" w:hAnsi="Arial"/><w:color w:val="2C2C2A"/><w:sz w:val="22"/><w:szCs w:val="22"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Each pillar contributes an independent probability/confidence score for a given 30-second window. Windows above a confidence threshold are used for health metric computation and baseline updates; windows below threshold are logged as &apos;household activity period&apos; and excluded â€” not
